# French fries

<section class="hmi-quick-read" id="french-fries-quick-read" data-hmi-quick-read aria-labelledby="french-fries-quick-read-label"><div class="hmi-quick-read-label" id="french-fries-quick-read-label">Quick read</div>

French fries in the HMI library has 89 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, uranium and Co from 9 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Le Gresley A, Ampem G, De Mars S, Grootveld M, Naughton DP · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">&quot;Real-World&quot; Evaluation of Lipid Oxidation Products and Trace Metals in French Fries From Two Chain Fast-Food Restaurants</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Frontiers in Nutrition 2021, Volume 8, Article 620952</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals|5]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Dabeka RW, McKenzie AD · 1995</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals">Survey of Lead, Cadmium, Fluoride, Nickel, and Cobalt in Food Composites and Estimation of Dietary Intakes of These Elements by Canadians in 1986-1988</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of AOAC International</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/cantoral2024-cadmium-foodstuffs-mexico|6]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Cantoral A, Collado-López S, Betanzos-Robledo L, Lamadrid-Figueroa H, García-Martínez BA, Ríos C, Díaz-Ruiz A, Mariscal-Moreno RM, Téllez-Rojo MM · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cantoral2024-cadmium-foodstuffs-mexico">Dietary Risk Assessment of Cadmium Exposure Through Commonly Consumed Foodstuffs in Mexico</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Foods</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cantoral2024-cadmium-foodstuffs-mexico">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

<ul>
<li>
<strong>Metals measured:</strong>

Lead, cadmium, total arsenic, total mercury, nickel and chromium and others, in 89 results from 9 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Le Gresley A, Ampem G, De Mars S, Grootveld M, Naughton DP · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">&quot;Real-World&quot; Evaluation of Lipid Oxidation Products and Trace Metals in French Fries From Two Chain Fast-Food Restaurants</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Frontiers in Nutrition 2021, Volume 8, Article 620952</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Main drivers:</strong>

French fries, oil from fast-food restaurant chain X, UK, Friday evening had lead higher at 179 µg/kg, against 166 µg/kg for fast-food restaurant chain X, UK, Friday afternoon in the same study.<sup class="cite">[[sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Le Gresley A, Ampem G, De Mars S, Grootveld M, Naughton DP · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">&quot;Real-World&quot; Evaluation of Lipid Oxidation Products and Trace Metals in French Fries From Two Chain Fast-Food Restaurants</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Frontiers in Nutrition 2021, Volume 8, Article 620952</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Practical framing:</strong>

Ask for lot results with a quantification limit well below the limit that applies in the destination market.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Le Gresley A, Ampem G, De Mars S, Grootveld M, Naughton DP · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">&quot;Real-World&quot; Evaluation of Lipid Oxidation Products and Trace Metals in French Fries From Two Chain Fast-Food Restaurants</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Frontiers in Nutrition 2021, Volume 8, Article 620952</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
</ul>

French fries, oil from fast-food restaurant chain X, UK, Saturday morning had lead higher at 355 µg/kg, against 166 µg/kg for fast-food restaurant chain X, UK, Friday afternoon in the same study.<sup class="cite">[[sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Le Gresley A, Ampem G, De Mars S, Grootveld M, Naughton DP · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">&quot;Real-World&quot; Evaluation of Lipid Oxidation Products and Trace Metals in French Fries From Two Chain Fast-Food Restaurants</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Frontiers in Nutrition 2021, Volume 8, Article 620952</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the french fries came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.



<a id="ingredient-identity-and-scope"></a>
## What counts as french fries

<a id="hmi-ing-001"></a>
<a id="what-commodity-biological-species-edible-part-and-commercial-forms-does-this-page-cover"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-002"></a>
<a id="which-processing-states-and-derivatives-are-distinct-materials-rather-than-synonyms"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-003"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-can-be-transferred-between-those-materials-and-what-requires-a-separate-answer"></a>

This page covers french fries in 3 forms kept separate in every comparison: french fries, as sold (62 results), french fries, oil (26 results) and french fries, flour or meal (1 result).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Le Gresley A, Ampem G, De Mars S, Grootveld M, Naughton DP · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">&quot;Real-World&quot; Results stay on the basis each source reports: as sold or fresh weight, dry weight, or unstated. Dry-weight, as-sold and unstated results are never pooled, and no moisture conversion is applied.

<div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-french-fries" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-french-fries-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of french fries, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

Different HMI scopes are not one common ranking.</p></details></div></details><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-baseline-fallback id="hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:french-fries-90e0f43eec4c:d28e718b7856"><summary>HMI french fries U reference · French fries, as sold</summary><div data-baseline-reference-template="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:french-fries-90e0f43eec4c:d28e718b7856"><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Reference and scope</p><p>HMI french fries U reference · French fries, as sold. French fries, as sold; As sold, as eaten or fresh weight, µg/kg; no moisture conversion. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>1.1</td><td>1.8</td><td>2.4</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>1.1</td><td>1.75</td><td>2.3</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>27 reference units; 1 sampling family. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>FDA Total Diet Study</td><td>27</td><td>100%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for french fries, as sold on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>FDA Total Diet Study is the only compatible sampling family for french fries, as sold on this basis in the completed french fries ledger, so this single-family reference cannot represent other markets. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020" class="internal" data-slug="sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020" data-no-cite-meta>FDA Total Diet Study</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. Method: hmi-pooled-reference-v2.</p><p>hmi-pooled-reference-v2:french-fries-90e0f43eec4c:d28e718b7856</p><p>The reference is computed by HMI from admitted evidence, not copied from a study baseline. It describes the evidence currently in this scope; it is not a worldwide or current-lot benchmark. Source, year and region filters do not change it. Different HMI scopes are not one common ranking.</p></details></div></details><div hidden data-form-reasons><p data-reason="no_numeric_value_or_bound" data-title="Spread or extreme value only">The source reports only a percentile, range or maximum for these samples, with no mean or median to place against the reference. Spread figures that do have a mean or median are shown on that row instead.</p><p data-reason="bounds_span_categories" data-title="Non-detect spans the bands">The result is below a reporting limit, and the range from zero to that limit crosses more than one band.</p><p data-reason="basis_incompatible_with_all_references" data-title="Basis has no reference">The result is on a basis (dry weight, or not stated) that no HMI reference for this form uses, so it cannot be placed.</p></div><script type="application/octet-stream" data-explorer-payload class="hmi-selected-result" data-selected-finding><strong class="hmi-finding">median &lt;5 µg/kg (below reporting limits)</strong><span class="hmi-row-note">median.</span><p>52 results. Basis: As sold, as eaten or fresh weight.</p><p>Non-detects are intervals from zero to the reporting limit; never a measured zero</p></div><div class="hmi-selected-comparison" data-selected-comparison><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">HMI baseline position</p><p>Limited data · HMI french fries Pb reference · French fries, as sold. median as reported: ≤5 µg/kg. HMI median ≤4; Q1 ≤4; Q3 ≤5 µg/kg. 2 families. Reporting bounds prevent a rating.</p><p>Reporting bounds or insufficient reference spread prevent a stable Low / Moderate / High classification.</p><p>This finding’s sampling family contributes to the fixed reference.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sensitivity to weighting</p><p>Equal weight per subgroup: Limited data. The position remains unresolved under this alternative. This check does not address all sampling, rounding or analytical uncertainty.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sensitivity to source selection</p><ul><li>Without WHO GEMS/Food: Limited data.</li><li>Without FDA Total Diet Study: Limited data.</li></ul><p>These checks show dependence on each sampling family; they are not confidence intervals.</p><div data-baseline-reference="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:french-fries-6b4ce74c2fa2:a2df487baa8a"><a href="#hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:french-fries-6b4ce74c2fa2:a2df487baa8a">Reference, contributing evidence and methods</a></div></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Collection period</p><p data-timeline-scope>2004–2017</p><div data-selected-timeline><p>No year-by-year sample register is reconstructed from this summary.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Geographic scope</p><p data-regional-scope>EURO</p><div data-selected-regional><p>Purchase or reporting market; growing area not identified.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sampling, analysis and reporting limits</p><p>As described by the monitoring programme.</p><p>Non-detects are intervals from zero to the reporting limit; never a measured zero</p><p></p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Shared samples and study overlap</p><p>Subgroup rows from the same source share its laboratory and sampling frame; they are not independent studies.</p><p>Each table row describes a food/analyte result. Repeated rows do not add independent samples or datasets. <a id="sources-and-contamination-pathways"></a>
## How metals reach french fries

<a id="hmi-ing-010"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-root-uptake-surface-deposition-feed-transfer-or-other-biological-pathways"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-011"></a>
<a id="what-contamination-can-arise-from-agricultural-inputs-harvesting-processing-equipment-or-packaging"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-012"></a>
<a id="which-source-attribution-claims-are-measured-modelled-inferred-or-unresolved"></a>

The evidence on how metals reach french fries comes from studies that compared exposed and ordinary settings. French fries, oil from fast-food restaurant chain X, UK, Saturday morning had lead higher at 355 µg/kg, against 166 µg/kg for fast-food restaurant chain X, UK, Friday afternoon in the same study.<sup class="cite">[[sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Le Gresley A, Ampem G, De Mars S, Grootveld M, Naughton DP · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">&quot;Real-World&quot; Evaluation of Lipid Oxidation Products and Trace Metals in French Fries From Two Chain Fast-Food Restaurants</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Frontiers in Nutrition 2021, Volume 8, Article 620952</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

These are observational comparisons from single studies; the size of the difference depends on the site, metal and distance.



<a id="ranges-by-source-region-and-variety"></a>
## Which forms and regions measured lower

<a id="hmi-ing-013"></a>
<a id="how-do-concentrations-vary-by-origin-growing-conditions-variety-and-harvest-period"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-014"></a>

<a id="hmi-ing-015"></a>
<a id="which-comparisons-support-a-sourcing-decision-and-where-would-geographic-or-supplier-generalization-be-unjustified"></a>

For cadmium, the HMI reference median was lowest for french fries, as sold (43 µg/kg) and highest for french fries, flour or meal (47.5 µg/kg); forms differ in water content, so this compares foods as sold or eaten, not uptake.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS median of below 4 µg/kg and a middle half spanning below 4 (lower quartile) and below 5 µg/kg (upper quartile).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" was 43 µg/kg, with a middle half from 32.6 to 58 µg/kg across 40 units.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span to 61 µg/kg across 17 units.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Among GEMS/Food reporting regions, french fries, as sold had the lowest cadmium subgroup median in EURO (22.5 µg/kg) and the highest in EURO (47.6 µg/kg); reporting regions are markets, not growing areas.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

In one study, lead across 6 groups of french fries ranged from 66.4 µg/kg (fast-food restaurant chain Y, UK, Saturday daily average) to 210 µg/kg (fast-food restaurant chain X, UK, Saturday daily average); the lowest is the lowest observed in that study, not an established sourcing preference.<sup class="cite">[[sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Le Gresley A, Ampem G, De Mars S, Grootveld M, Naughton DP · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">&quot;Real-World&quot; Evaluation of Lipid Oxidation Products and Trace Metals in French Fries From Two Chain Fast-Food Restaurants</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Frontiers in Nutrition 2021, Volume 8, Article 620952</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

<a id="trade-offs-and-failure-modes"></a>
## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="hmi-ing-022"></a>
<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-023"></a>
<a id="what-effects-on-nutrition-yield-sensory-quality-stability-and-waste-streams-were-measured"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-024"></a>
<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

Avoiding exposed growing areas lowered the metal measured in the studies available, but no study measured the cost or supply effect of doing so.<sup class="cite">[[sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Le Gresley A, Ampem G, De Mars S, Grootveld M, Naughton DP · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">&quot;Real-World&quot; Evaluation of Lipid Oxidation Products and Trace Metals in French Fries From Two Chain Fast-Food Restaurants</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Frontiers in Nutrition 2021, Volume 8, Article 620952</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes a lead limit says little about cadmium, arsenic or nickel unless those are tested as well.

Results depend on the method and its reporting limit. Reporting limits for lead ranged from 0.4 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 200 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a <a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## French fries FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-french-fries" data-anchor="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-french-fries" data-question="What heavy metals are found in french fries?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What heavy metals are found in french fries?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The library reports lead, cadmium, total arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, uranium and Co in french fries, most often lead, cadmium and total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Le Gresley A, Ampem G, De Mars S, Grootveld M, Naughton DP · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">&quot;Real-World&quot; Evaluation of Lipid Oxidation Products and Trace Metals in French Fries From Two Chain Fast-Food Restaurants</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Frontiers in Nutrition 2021, Volume 8, Article 620952</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported lead in french fries, as sold from 94 results in 2 reporting regions, with a subgroup median of 2.7 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="how-do-heavy-metals-get-into-french-fries" data-anchor="how-do-heavy-metals-get-into-french-fries" data-question="How do heavy metals get into french fries?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>How do heavy metals get into french fries?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

In the studies available, french fries from contaminated or exposed settings carried more metal than french fries from the same study’s control.<sup class="cite">[[sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Le Gresley A, Ampem G, De Mars S, Grootveld M, Naughton DP · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">&quot;Real-World&quot; Evaluation of Lipid Oxidation Products and Trace Metals in French Fries From Two Chain Fast-Food Restaurants</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Frontiers in Nutrition 2021, Volume 8, Article 620952</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> French fries, oil from fast-food restaurant chain X, UK, Friday evening had lead higher at 179 µg/kg, against 166 µg/kg for fast-food restaurant chain X, UK, Friday afternoon in the same study.<sup class="cite">[[sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Le Gresley A, Ampem G, De Mars S, Grootveld M, Naughton DP · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">&quot;Real-World&quot; Evaluation of Lipid Oxidation Products and Trace Metals in French Fries From Two Chain Fast-Food Restaurants</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Frontiers in Nutrition 2021, Volume 8, Article 620952</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/legresley2021-uk-fast-food-french-fries-lead">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="which-types-of-french-fries-have-the-lowest-levels" data-anchor="which-types-of-french-fries-have-the-lowest-levels" data-question="Which types of french fries have the lowest levels?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Which types of french fries have the lowest levels?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

For cadmium, the HMI reference median was lowest for french fries, as sold (43 µg/kg) and highest for french fries, flour or meal (47.5 µg/kg); forms differ in water content, so this compares foods as sold or eaten, not uptake.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="which-regions-report-the-lowest-levels-in-french-fries" data-anchor="which-regions-report-the-lowest-levels-in-french-fries" data-question="Which regions report the lowest levels in french fries?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Which regions report the lowest levels in french fries?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Among GEMS/Food reporting regions, french fries, as sold had the lowest cadmium subgroup median in EURO (22.5 µg/kg) and the highest in EURO (47.6 µg/kg); reporting regions are markets, not growing areas.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>
